[tool-c26] exec_command <- Inspect x in 1200-3000 region ($ cd /app && python3 - <<'PY' import numpy as np rows = [] with open('graphene.dat', 'r', newline='') as f: for line in f: line = line.strip() if not line: continue parts = line.split('\t') if len(parts) != 2: continue x = float(parts[0].replace(',', '.')) y = float(parts[1].replace(',', '.')) rows.append((x, y)) rows = np.array(rows) x, y = rows[:,0], rows[:,1] order = np.argsort(x) xs, ys = x[order], y[order] # Find local maxima in this region mask = (xs > 1100) & (xs < 3000) sub_x, sub_y = xs[mask], ys[mask] print("num points in [1100,3000]:", len(sub_x)) print("x range:", sub_x.min(), sub_x.max()) print("y range:", sub_y.min(), sub_y.max()) # find local maxima peaks = [] for i in range(1, len(sub_y)-1): if sub_y[i] >= sub_y[i-1] and sub_y[i] >= sub_y[i+1]: peaks.append((sub_x[i], sub_y[i])) peaks = sorted(peaks, key=lambda t: t[0]) print("\nLocal maxima between 1100 and 3000:") for p in peaks: print(f"x={p[0]:.3f} y={p[1]:.3f}") # Print sorted table in region 1400-2800 to see structure print("\nSelected data (x, y) ascending:") for i in range(0, len(sub_x)): if 1400 <= sub_x[i] <= 2800 and i % 5 == 0: pass # Print first ~120 points of the relevant band around 1400-2800 idx = np.argsort(xs) sel = np.where((xs > 1400) & (xs < 2800))[0] print("\n--- points with x in (1400,2800) (every 3rd) ---") for j in sel[::3]: print(f"{xs[j]:.2f}, {ys[j]:.2f}") PY)

Let me write a Python script using scipy to:

[tool-633] exec_command <- Peak finding after baseline subtraction ($ cd /app && python3 - <<'PY' import numpy as np from scipy.signal import find_peaks from scipy.ndimage import minimum_filter1d rows = [] with open('graphene.dat', 'r', newline='') as f: for line in f: line = line.strip() if not line: continue parts = line.split('\t') if len(parts) != 2: continue x = float(parts[0].replace(',', '.')) y = float(parts[1].replace(',', '.')) rows.append((x, y)) rows = np.array(rows) order = np.argsort(rows[:,0]) xs, ys = rows[order,0], rows[order,1] # Baseline: rolling minimum over a window, then smooth window = 101 base = minimum_filter1d(ys, size=window, mode='nearest') resid = ys - base # Find peaks in residual with prominence threshold peaks, props = find_peaks(resid, prominence=200, distance=10) print("=== Peaks in residual (smoothed baseline) ===") data = sorted([(xs[p], resid[p], ys[p], p) for p in peaks], key=lambda t: -t[1]) for x, r, y, idx in data[:40]: print(f"x={x:.3f} resid={r:10.2f} y={y:10.2f} idx={idx}") print("\n=== All peaks with resid > 300, sorted by x === ") data2 = sorted([(xs[p], resid[p], ys[p], p) for p in peaks if resid[p] > 300], key=lambda t: t[0]) for x, r, y, idx in data2: print(f"x={x:10.3f} resid={r:10.2f} y={y:10.2f}") # Also compute simple derivative-based peaks in raw data around candidate regions print("\n=== Candidate graphene regions ===") for lo, hi in [(1500,1700),(2500,2800),(15500,18000),(16000,16800)]: m = (xs >= lo) & (xs <= hi) if m.sum() > 0: j = np.argmax(ys[m]) xm = xs[m][j]; ym = ys[m][j] print(f"Region [{lo},{hi}]: max y={ym:.2f} at x={xm:.3f}") PY)

[tool-1fa] exec_command <- Smoothed analysis of low-x region ($ cd /app && python3 - <<'PY' import numpy as np from scipy.signal import savgol_filter, find_peaks rows = [] with open('graphene.dat', 'r', newline='') as f: for line in f: line = line.strip() if not line: continue parts = line.split('\t') if len(parts) != 2: continue x = float(parts[0].replace(',', '.')) y = float(parts[1].replace(',', '.')) rows.append((x, y)) rows = np.array(rows) order = np.argsort(rows[:,0]) xs, ys = rows[order,0], rows[order,1] # Region where x corresponds to plausible Raman shifts 1200-3200 mask = (xs >= 1200) & (xs <= 3400) xx, yy = xs[mask], ys[mask] # Smooth with savgol window = 51 sm = savgol_filter(yy, window, 3) print("Points in [1200,3400]:", len(xx)) # Find peaks in smoothed peaks, props = find_peaks(sm, prominence=50, distance=20) data = sorted([(xx[p], sm[p], props['prominences'][k]) for k,p in enumerate(peaks)], key=lambda t: -t[1]) print("\nTop 20 peaks in smoothed [1200,3400]:") for x, y, p in data[:20]: print(f"x={x:8.2f} smoothed_y={y:8.2f} prom={p:8.2f}") # Also check symmetric: maybe x axis values relate as Raman shift = laser - x. # Let's identify overall max structure: find peaks over whole ascending range with smoothing print("\n=== Full spectrum smoothed peaks (top 15) ===") ws = 51 sm_all = savgol_filter(ys, ws, 3) peaks_all, props_all = find_peaks(sm_all, prominence=100, distance=20) data_all = sorted([(xs[p], sm_all[p], props_all['prominences'][k]) for k,p in enumerate(peaks_all)], key=lambda t: -t[1]) for x, y, p in data_all[:15]: print(f"x={x:10.2f} smoothed_y={y:10.2f} prom={p:10.2f}") PY)

Let me be careful about the Lorentzian normalization. Two common forms:
1. f = offset + A * γ^2 / ((x-x0)^2 + γ^2)  → peak value = offset + A, HWHM = γ.
2. f = offset + A / (1 + ((x-x0)/γ)^2)  → same as #1.
Both give same A/γ/x0. Good.

[tool-6b6] exec_command <- Fit Lorentzian to G and 2D peaks ($ cd /app && python3 - <<'PY' import numpy as np from scipy.optimize import curve_fit import json rows = [] with open('graphene.dat', 'r', newline='') as f: for line in f: line = line.strip() if not line: continue parts = line.split('\t') if len(parts) != 2: continue x = float(parts[0].replace(',', '.')) y = float(parts[1].replace(',', '.')) rows.append((x, y)) rows = np.array(rows) order = np.argsort(rows[:,0]) xs, ys = rows[order,0], rows[order,1] def lorentzian(x, x0, gamma, amplitude, offset): return offset + amplitude * gamma**2 / ((x - x0)**2 + gamma**2) def fit_peak(x, y, x0_guess, gamma_guess, amp_guess, offset_guess=1000): p0 = [x0_guess, gamma_guess, amp_guess, offset_guess] popt, pcov = curve_fit(lorentzian, x, y, p0=p0, maxfev=10000) perr = np.sqrt(np.diag(pcov)) return popt, perr # G peak region g_mask = (xs >= 3680) & (xs <= 3850) gx, gy = xs[g_mask], ys[g_mask] popt_g, perr_g = fit_peak(gx, gy, 3747, 6, 9000, 2500) print("G fit:") print(" x0 =", popt_g[0], "+/-", perr_g[0]) print(" gamma =", popt_g[1], "+/-", perr_g[1]) print(" amp =", popt_g[2], "+/-", perr_g[2]) print(" offset =", popt_g[3], "+/-", perr_g[3]) # 2D peak region d_mask = (xs >= 6240) & (xs <= 6450) dx, dy = xs[d_mask], ys[d_mask] popt_d, perr_d = fit_peak(dx, dy, 6320, 15, 6000, 7000) print("\n2D fit:") print(" x0 =", popt_d[0], "+/-", perr_d[0]) print(" gamma =", popt_d[1], "+/-", perr_d[1]) print(" amp =", popt_d[2], "+/-", perr_d[2]) print(" offset =", popt_d[3], "+/-", perr_d[3]) # Compute residuals (chi2) for sanity res_g = gy - lorentzian(gx, *popt_g) res_d = dy - lorentzian(dx, *popt_d) print("\nG residual std:", np.std(res_g), " max:", np.abs(res_g).max()) print("2D residual std:", np.std(res_d), " max:", np.abs(res_d).max()) print("G peak model max:", popt_g[3]+popt_g[2], " raw max:", gy.max()) print("2D peak model max:", popt_d[3]+popt_d[2], " raw max:", dy.max()) PY)

[tool-a37] exec_command <- Robust Lorentzian fit with tail-based baseline ($ cd /app && python3 - <<'PY' import numpy as np from scipy.optimize import curve_fit rows = [] with open('graphene.dat', 'r', newline='') as f: for line in f: line = line.strip() if not line: continue parts = line.split('\t') if len(parts) != 2: continue x = float(parts[0].replace(',', '.')) y = float(parts[1].replace(',', '.')) rows.append((x, y)) rows = np.array(rows) order = np.argsort(rows[:,0]) xs, ys = rows[order,0], rows[order,1] def lor(x, x0, gamma, amp): return amp * gamma**2 / ((x - x0)**2 + gamma**2) def fit_tail(window, tail_left, tail_right, x0_guess, gamma_guess, amp_guess): mask = (xs >= window[0]) & (xs <= window[1]) x, y = xs[mask], ys[mask] # baseline from tails base_mask = (x <= tail_left) | (x >= tail_right) baseline = np.mean(y[base_mask]) ysub = y - baseline popt, pcov = curve_fit(lor, x, ysub, p0=[x0_guess, gamma_guess, amp_guess], maxfev=20000) perr = np.sqrt(np.diag(pcov)) resid = ysub - lor(x, *popt) return dict(x0=popt[0], gamma=popt[1], amp=popt[2], offset=baseline, x0err=perr[0], gammaerr=perr[1], amperr=perr[2], resid_std=np.std(resid), n_pts=len(x), tails=basi) # G fits with a few window/tail choices print("=== G peak (center ~3745) ===") for win, tl, tr in [([3690,3800], 3700, 3792), ([3680,3820], 3695, 3805), ([3710,3780], 3712, 3778)]: try: r = fit_tail(win, tl, tr, 3745, 6, 9000) print(f"win={win} tails({tl},{tr}): x0={r['x0']:.3f}+/-{r['x0err']:.3f}, gamma={r['gamma']:.3f}+/-{r['gammaerr']:.3f}, amp={r['amp']:.1f}, offset={r['offset']:.1f}, resid_std={r['resid_std']:.1f}") except Exception as e: print("err", win, e) print("\n=== 2D peak (center ~6327) ===") for win, tl, tr in [([6250,6420], 6260, 6410), ([6280,6400], 6290, 6390), ([6240,6450], 6250, 6440)]: try: r = fit_tail(win, tl, tr, 6327, 16, 7000) print(f"win={win} tails({tl},{tr}): x0={r['x0']:.3f}+/-{r['x0err']:.3f}, gamma={r['gamma']:.3f}+/-{r['gammaerr']:.3f}, amp={r['amp']:.1f}, offset={r['offset']:.1f}, resid_std={r['resid_std']:.1f}") except Exception as e: print("err", win, e) PY)

Design decisions for the script:

```python
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

def load_spectrum(path):
    xs, ys = [], []
    with open(path) as f:
        for line in f:
            line = line.strip()
            if not line: continue
            a, b = line.split()
            xs.append(float(a.replace(',', '.')))
            ys.append(float(b.replace(',', '.')))
    return np.array(xs), np.array(ys)

x, y = load_spectrum('graphene.dat')

fig, axs = plt.subplots(3, 1, figsize=(? , ?))
```
